Remark 4.5

(One-point function allocation)
.

In Figure
10
, we have ten connections, nine of which can be bounded by
\tilde{G}_{z}
, and one by
\bar{G}_{z}(k)
.
In general, the diagram of
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}
has
4N+2
connections, and only one of them needs to be bounded by
\bar{G}_{z}
.
This means that by bounding each line by
\bar{G}_{z}
instead of
\tilde{G}_{z}
, as done previously in literature,
any bound on
\hat{\Xi}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}
is unnecessarily a factor
{\mathrm{e}}^{4N+1}
too large, at least for
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0
.
For
\underline{b}_{1}=0
three lines are trivial (
I_{S2}=I_{S3}=I_{S4}=\varnothing
), so that this unnecessary factor is still
{\mathrm{e}}^{4N-2}
.
We can and will choose which of the lines is bounded by
\bar{G}_{z}(k)
in a way that sensitively depends on the precise structure of a diagram.
In Section
6.2
, we explain in detail how we do this for the lace-expansion coefficients with the spatial weight
|x|^{2}
,
for which it is the most relevant. For bounds without the spatial weights, we usually use the split shown in Figure
11
.
There, we use the bound
\bar{G}_{z}(x)\leq g^{\iota}_{z}\tilde{G}_{z}(x)
for
x\neq 0
for the single
\bar{G}_{z}
factor, which simply extracts one
one-point function
g^{\iota}_{z}
, after which all connections of the diagram are given by
\tilde{G}_{z}
.

4.3
Repulsive diagrams

In this section, we define the basic diagrams used to bound the NoBLE coefficients.
The ribs/sausages of the NoBLE coefficients have numerous avoidance constraints.
We incorporate some of these in our diagrams to improve our numerical bounds.
Here we explain how this can be done using so-called
repulsive diagrams
.

To motivate the precise definition of these diagrams, we review the avoidance structure of the first square
(1,2,3,4)
in Figure
11
.
In the diagrams the vertices
x_{i}
denote points on the backbone, the vertices
w_{i}
denote the
i
th first intersection point (recall Definition
4.4
), and
u_{i}
the vertices where we have to split a sausage/rib.
Alike the split of
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}
given above, the split given in Figure
11
has the following interpretation:

\rhd

Line
2
is the connected planted animal
B^{\omega}_{0}(0,w_{1})
;

\rhd

Line
3
is the connected planted animal
B^{\omega}_{t_{1}}(x_{1},w_{1})
;

\rhd

Line
4
is the connected planted animal
B^{\omega}_{t_{1}}(w_{1},u_{1})
;

\rhd

Line
1
is the backbone
b^{\omega}(0,x_{1})
, together with the sausages
(A^{\omega}_{i})_{i=1,\dots,t_{1}-1}
and the (trimmed) animal

A^{\omega}_{t_{1}}\setminus(B^{\omega}_{t_{1}}(x_{1},w_{1})\cup B^{\omega}_{t_{1}}(w_{1},u_{1}))
.

These lines obey the following avoidance constraints:

\rhd

Relation between
1
and
2
: By the definition of
J[0,|\omega|]
, the sausage
A_{t_{1}}^{\omega}
is the first sausage to intersect with the sausage
A_{0}^{\omega}
.
Thus, lines
2
and
1
only have the origin as starting point in common and are otherwise vertex-disjoint;

\rhd

Relation between
1
and
3,4
: The connections
3
and
4
are all in
A_{t_{1}}^{\omega}
.
The indicator
J[0,|\omega|]
ensures that
A_{t_{1}}^{\omega}
does not intersect any of the intermediate sausages
(A_{i}^{\omega})_{i=1,2,\dots,t_{1}-1}
.
Further,
1
bounds the part of
A_{t_{1}}^{\omega}
that has not been split off as planted animal into 3, indicated by the tip of the arrow in Figure
11
.
Thus,
1
is bond-disjoint from
3
and
4
;

\rhd

Relation between
3
and
4
: Both are part of the sausage
A_{t_{1}}^{\omega}
and are thus bond-disjoint;

\rhd

Relation between
2
and
3,4
: We know that
A_{0}^{\omega}
and
A_{t_{1}}^{\omega}
intersect in at least one point.
We choose
w_{1}=w_{1}(\omega)
to be the
first intersection point
, as defined in Definition
4.4
.
By this choice the backbones
b^{A^{\omega}_{0}}(0,w_{1})
and
b^{A^{\omega}_{t_{1}}}(x_{1},w_{1})
only intersect at
w_{1}
.

We define repulsive diagrams to bound such diagrams. As seen for
2
and
3,4
, the constraint that the pieces are bond-disjoint is too strong.
For this reason, we denote by repulsiveness of a diagram that we can find bond-disjoint paths connecting the corner points of the diagram.

In the following, we define the
skeleton
of a diagram that encodes the avoidance constraints of the backbone lines, the lengths of the backbone lines
and the information whether the start or the end points of a connection is the root of a planted animal. This is formalized as follows:

Definition 4.6

(Mutually avoiding skeleton)
.

Let
x_{0}=0\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
n\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. For each
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, let
x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},l_{i}\in\Nbold
, the index
j_{i}\in\{l_{i},\underline{l}_{i}\}
,
s_{i}\in\{+,-\}
, and let
\omega^{i}
be a sausage-walk from
x_{i-1}
to
x_{i}
. We define
S^{s_{1},\dots,s_{n}}_{j_{1},\dots,j_{n}}(\omega^{1},\dots,\omega^{n})
to be the indicator that the following holds:

(1)

There exists a sequence of paths
(p^{i})_{i=1}^{n}
such that
p^{i}
is a path from
x_{i-1}
to
x_{i}
and
p^{i}
uses only edges in
\omega^{i}
, each path
p^{i}
describes a self-avoiding walk and all paths are pairwise bond-disjoint;

(2)

For each
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, we can choose
p^{i}
in item (1) such that,
|p^{i}|\geq l_{i}
when
j_{i}=l_{i}
, while
|p^{i}|=l_{i}
when
j_{i}=\underline{l}_{i}
;

(3)

For each
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the sausages of the walk
(A^{\omega^{i}}_{j})_{j=0,\dots,|\omega^{i}|}
do not intersect, so that
\omega^{i}
describes a LA.

(4)

For each
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
,
if
s_{i}=+
then
A^{\omega^{i}}_{0}=\varnothing
, while
A^{\omega^{i}}_{|\omega^{i}|}=\varnothing
when
s_{i}=-
.

We next use this skeleton to define the repulsive bubble, triangle and square diagrams as follows:

Definition 4.7

(Repulsive diagrams)
.

For
i=1,2,3,4
let
x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\ l_{i}\in\Nbold,\ j_{i}\in\{l_{i},\underline{l}_{i}\}
and
x_{0}=0
. We define

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{j_{1},j_{2}}

\displaystyle(x_{1},x_{2})=\max_{s_{1},s_{2}\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{\omega^{1}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{0},x_{1})}\sum_{\omega^{2}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{1},x_{2})}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2}z^{|\omega^{i}|}Z[0,|\omega^{i}|]\right)S^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(\omega^{1},\omega^{2}),

(4.17)

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}

\displaystyle(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\omega^{1}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{0},x_{1})\\
\omega^{2}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{1},x_{2})\\
\omega^{3}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{2},x_{3})\end{array}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}z^{|\omega^{i}|}Z[0,|\omega^{i}|]\right)S^{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}(\omega^{1},\omega^{2},\omega^{3}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}

\displaystyle(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4}\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{\omega^{1}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{0},x_{1})}\sum_{\omega^{2}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{1},x_{2})}\sum_{\omega^{3}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{2},x_{3})}\sum_{\omega^{4}\in\mathcal{W}(x_{3},x_{4})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}z^{|\omega^{i}|}Z[0,|\omega^{i}|]\right)S^{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4}}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}(\omega^{1},\omega^{2},\omega^{3},\omega^{4}).

(4.21)

Further, for
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{i}(x)=\sum_{A\colon 0,x\in A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{d_{A}(0,x)\geq i\}}z^{|A|}.

(4.22)

Each line of these repulsive diagrams in (
4.17
), (
4.7
) and (
4.21
) are planted animals and can therefore be bounded using the modified two-point functions
\tilde{G}_{z}
. The double connection in (
4.22
) is special, and we will treat this separately in Section
4.4
below.

To use the constraints on the lengths of connections arising from loops consisting of at least 4 bonds in the NoBLE, we further define, for
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in{l,\underline{l}}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{j,z}(x)=\sum_{\omega\in\mathcal{W}(x_{0},x)}z^{|\omega|}Z[0,|\omega|]S^{+}_{j}(\omega),

(4.23)

and note that
\tilde{G}_{0,z}(x)=\tilde{G}_{z}(x)
.
Using
\tilde{G}_{j,z}
, we can obviously bound the repulsive diagrams by ignoring the self-avoidance constraints as

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{j_{1},z}(x_{1})\tilde{G}_{j_{2},z}(x_{2}-x_{1})\tilde{G}_{j_{3},z}(x_{2}-x_{3}).

(4.24)

However, as demonstrated by the fourth improvement in Section
4.1
, the use of
repulsiveness improves our numerical bounds drastically, so we do not use the worse bounds as in (
4.24
).

4.4
Bounds on double connections

The bound on contributions in which two-points are doubly connected, that is, connected by two-bond disjoint paths,
is of central interest for LAs. This is especially relevant since a large contribution to the NoBLE lace-expansion coefficients for LAs is given by

\displaystyle\bar{\Xi}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{z}(x)=\sum_{A\colon 0,x\in A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}z^{|A|}.

(4.25)

Using our notation we bound this
\mathscr{D}_{0}(x)
, defined in (
4.22
).

Let us briefly discuss a numerical aspect of our bounds. Using the techniques described in the previous section, we obtain

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{z}(x)\leq\min\big\{2dz(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)\bar{G}_{z}(x),\tilde{G}_{z}(x)^{2}\big\}.

(4.26)

We now explain how we can improve this bound by a factor close to
2
. This is possible as the two paths that created the double connection are interchangeable, so that
(
4.26
) bounds each possible animal
twice
.

For the technical derivation of this improvement, we consider yet another split of the LA
A
given as follows:

(i)

Let
p_{1}=(b^{1}_{1},b^{1}_{2},\dots,b^{1}_{n})
and
p_{2}(0,x)=(b^{2}_{1},b^{2}_{2},\dots,b^{2}_{m})
be two bond-disjoint paths between
0
and
x
in
A
,
that we choose in some unique way for each
A
, e.g.
p_{1}
is the smallest path in the lexicographic order such that there exists such a bond-disjoint
p_{2}
, and then
p_{2}
is the smallest of these bond-disjoint paths, so that
p_{1}
is smaller in lexicographic order than
p_{2}
as we will assume from now on.

(ii)

Let
A_{1}=B^{A}(0,b^{1}_{n};p_{1})
.

Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{A\colon 0,x\in A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}z^{|A|}=

\displaystyle\sum_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}z^{|A_{1}|}z^{|A\setminus A_{1}|}\leq 2dz(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)\bar{G}_{z}(x).

(4.27)

By symmetry, we could also have performed the split of the animal
A
according to the path
p_{2}
, instead.

We next improve upon this bound. Since the paths
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are bond-disjoint, we conclude that
\overline{b}^{1}_{1}={e}_{\iota}\neq\overline{b}^{2}_{1}={e}_{\kappa}
, with
\iota<\kappa
since
p_{1}
is smaller than
p_{2}
in the lexicographic order. Remove the bonds in the paths
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
from
A
. That divides the LA into at most
n+m+2
disjoint LAs containing the vertices of
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
. Thus, now defining
A_{0}
the LA containing
0
,
A_{1}=B^{A^{\prime}}(b^{1}_{1},x;p_{1})\setminus A_{0}
, where
A^{\prime}
is the LA obtained from
A_{2}
by removing
p_{2}
and keeping the parts that do not contain vertices in
p_{2}
. Then, by definition of
B^{A^{\prime}}(b^{1}_{1},x;p_{1})
,
A_{1}
is disjoint from
p_{2}
, and the sausage at
\overline{b}_{1}^{1}
is trivial in
A_{1}
. Finally, define
A_{2}=A\setminus(A_{0}\cup A_{1})
(which contains the bonds in
p_{2}
). Note that, by construction, the sausage of
x
in
A_{2}
is trivial, so that
A_{2}
is a planted LA, while also the sausage at
0
is trivial. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{A\colon 0,x\in A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}z^{|A|}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota}\sum_{\kappa>\iota}\sum_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\overline{b}^{1}_{1}={e}_{\iota}\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\overline{b}^{2}_{1}={e}_{\kappa}\}}z^{|A_{0}|}z^{|A_{1}|}z^{|A\setminus(A_{0}\cup A_{1})|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota}\sum_{\kappa>\iota}z^{2}g^{\iota}_{z}\tilde{G}_{z}(x-{e}_{\iota})G_{z}(x-{e}_{\kappa})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(2dzg^{\iota}_{z})^{2}(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)(D\star\bar{G}_{z})(x),

(4.28)

as the bound (
4.27
) does not assume anything on the order of the paths. Here, in the first inequality,
z^{2}
corresponds to the weight of the bonds
b_{1}^{1},b_{1}^{2}
, the factor
g^{\iota}_{z}
comes from the LA
A_{0}
, the factor
\tilde{G}_{z}
from the LA
A_{1}
, and the factor
\bar{G}_{z}
from the LA
A_{2}
.
Using the same arguments we obtain the factor
1/2
for all other terms in (
4.26
). In our final numerical bounds, we will drastically improve upon this bound by using the repulsive nature of the two paths
p_{1},p_{2}
.

5
The diagrammatic bounds

In the preceding section we have explained the technical details of our method of bounding LT/LA diagrams.
In this section we first combine the repulsive diagrams (
4.17
)-(
4.21
), to larger diagrams that we call
building blocks
.
Then, we combine these building blocks to the create bounds on the NoBLE coefficients.
We close this section with an overview of where to find all bounds required for the analysis, as stated in
[
12
, Assumption 4.3]
.

In Section
6
we prove the bounds for
N=0
and sketch how the remaining bounds are proven in the example of
N=1,2
. The rigorous proof of these bounds is tedious and is based on techniques that are relatively standard in lace-expansion analyses. For this reason we omit many details.

5.1
Building blocks

In this section we combine the simple diagram, defined in (
4.17
)-(
4.21
), to larger diagrams, that we call
building blocks
. These building blocks are designed
to encode the following information:

\rhd

the repulsiveness of the paths involved;

\rhd

the lengths of lines shared by two loops;

\rhd

the role of the lines shared by two loops, meaning whether it is part of the backbone or not, and, if so, how the backbone is connected to the line in question.

We encode the repulsiveness by defining the blocks using the repulsive diagram (
4.17
)-(
4.21
). In the following we discuss the other two features.

Aim: encoding backbone-paths.

In Figure
11
, we have shown an example of the diagram contributing to
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})}_{z}
.
However, the form shown in Figure
11
is only one of eight possible patterns that the backbone can form. These patterns arise by the different cases for the underlying lace, i.e. whether
s_{i+1}=t_{i}
or
s_{i+1}<t_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
. In Figure
12
we show all the possible backbone patterns for the
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})}}
-diagrams. One function of the building block will be to encode all possible patterns at once, so as to encode the roles that the shared lines play compared to the backbone.

0
x
0
x
0
x
0
x
0
x
0
x
0
x
0
x

Figure 12
:
Picture of all possible
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})}}_{z}
diagrams.
The backbone is marked by a thick line and the four
first intersection points
are marked as dots.
The leftmost diagram on the second row corresponds to the diagram shown in Figure
11
.

Aim: encoding of the line lengths.

When creating a bound on the NoBLE coefficients we want to make full use of all the avoidance constraints of a sausage-walk, as well as the fact that any closed loop consists of at least four bonds. To be able to do this, we consider different cases for the lengths of paths that are shared by two squares. Here, the length of a path is given by the number of bonds it uses.

Our solution.

We use the indices
l,m\in\{-2,-1,0,1,2\}
to encode the information of the length and role of a shared line.
We explain their meaning at the example of the diagram given in Figure
13
.

0
m_{0}=-2
m_{1}=2
m_{2}=1
m_{3}=-2
m_{4}=0
m_{5}=-2
x

Figure 13
:
Example of a description of the backbone-path and the length of the shared line using the indices
(a_{i})_{i}
.

As indicated in Figures
11
,
12
and
13
, we always draw the diagrams in such a way that the origin is on the left side and the first piece of the sausage-walk from
\underline{b}_{0}
to
\underline{b}_{s_{2}}
is the lower line of the first square. A positive index
m_{i}
indicates that the backbone ends on the upper part of a piece of the diagram.
Interactions between neighboring squares will be particularly important, in which case there will be two indices, for example in
A^{m_{i-1},m_{i}}
. In this case,
m_{i-1}>0
indicates that the backbone in the
(i-1)
st square starts on the upper part of the diagram, while
m_{i}>0
indicates that the backbone ends on the upper part of a piece of the diagram.
By
|m_{i}|=1
, we denote that the vertical line consists of exactly one bond and
|m_{i}|=2
denotes that
the vertical line consists of at least two bonds. If
m_{i}=0
, then the vertical line is trivial, i.e., it consists of no bonds.
In the case that
m_{i}=0
, we draw the following piece of the backbone on the lower part of the following square.

Informal definition.

In this section, we informally define the diagrams, also called
building blocks
, that we use to bound the NoBLE coefficients. The precise properties of the building blocks originate from the properties of the NoBLE coefficients, as we will see when we bound the coefficients in Section
6
. The list below consists of 9 basic building blocks:

(i.)

Open square
A^{l,m}
.

We use this building block to bound the intermediate pieces of the diagrams appearing in the NoBLE coefficients. It consists of an open square, with 4 of the 5 vertices fixed and one being summed over.
A^{l,m}(u,v,x,y):=\sum_{w}
l\leq 0

m\leq 0

We label the vertices in the diagram
A^{l,m}
such that
u
is the starting vertex and
x
is the ending vertex of the backbone.
The indices
l,m
identify the lengths of the connections between
u,v
and
x,y
, respectively, i.e., the number of bonds in these connections.
For example: If
m=0
then
x=y
. If
|m|=1
then
x
and
y
are direct neighbors and are directly connected. The case where
|m|=2
summarizes all the other cases.

A positive index
l,m
indicates that the backbone starts/ends at the top. This means that if
lm\geq 0
, then the connection
x
to
y
is not part of the backbone.
The horizontal parts of the backbones (i.e., the paths from
u
to
x
or from
u
to
y
) are always non-trivial, in that they use at least one bond.
If the backbone
b^{A}(u,x)
consists of only one bond, then we know that
w\not\in\{u,x\}
.
Closing the diagram, by including
u\longleftrightarrow v
, always creates a diagram with at least four bonds.

Double open triangle

. The role of

and

in
\bar{A}^{l,m}
is the same as for

.

:
For
m=0
, we have that
x=y
, apart from that, the diagram remains the same.

Each triangle, square and pentagon consists of at least four bonds. The first diagram characterizes the case where the first step of the backbone starts at the origin, i.e.,
\underline{b}_{1}^{\omega}=0
.
In the following, we use
u=\bar{b}_{1}^{\omega}
to denote the first step of the backbone. Note that only
\underline{b}_{1}^{\omega}=0
contributes for LT.
If the backbone between
u
and
x
consists of only one step, then
w\not\in\{u,x\}
.
The pieces of the backbone
b^{A}(u,x)
,
b^{A}(0,y)
and
b^{A}(x,y)
, respectively, are non-trivial.
Note that the left diagram is the same diagram as
A^{0,m}(0,0,x,y)
.

(v.)

Open fundamental block
\bar{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}.

This diagram corresponds to the closed fundamental block
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}
, in which the connection
x
to
y
does not contribute (but we keep information about its length using the index
m
).

(vi.)

Weighted fundamental block
\Delta^{\text{start},m}
.

This diagram corresponds to the open fundamental block
\bar{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}
, where the lower connection is weighted by its displacement, i.e.,
|x|^{2}
for
m\leq 0
and
|y|^{2}
for
m\geq 0
.

(vii.)

Weighted terminal block
\Delta^{\text{end},m}
.

This diagram is defined by
\Delta^{\text{end},m}(u,v,x)=C^{m,0}(u,v,x,x)
and is used to bound the last square of
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}
and
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}
.

(viii.)

Iota-fundamental block
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,m}.

This diagram is used to bound the first square of
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}
and has the following structure for
m<0
:
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,-m}(x,y):=\sum_{w}
\sum_{v,w,u,\underline{b}_{1}}
0
u
e_{\iota}
v
\underline{b}_{1}
m
y
x
w
+\sum_{v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}
0
e_{\iota}
\underline{b}_{1}
v
y
m
x
w

This diagram is similar to
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}
. The sausage-walks that we bound with this diagram have the property that either
{e}_{\iota}
is part of the first sausage or that
\underline{b}_{1}^{\omega}
ends in
{e}_{\iota}
. If
{e}_{\iota}
is in the first sausage, then we extend the idea of (
4.15
).
We identify the last point
v
that the double connection
0
to
\underline{b}_{1}
and a connection
0
to
w
share.
Then, we identify the last common point
u
of the connection
0
to
{e}_{\iota}
to the rest.
This point
u
could be on all possible connections
0
to
\underline{b}_{1}
,
0
to
v
,
\underline{b}_{1}
to
v
as well as
v
to
w
, as indicated in the figure above.

(ix.)

Weighted iota-fundamental block
\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m},\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}
.

These diagrams have the same shape as
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,m}
, but the connection
x
to
y
does not contribute. Further,
\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}
is weighted by a factor
|x|^{2}
and
\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}
by a factor
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
for
m\leq 0
.

The formal definition of these diagrams is given in Appendix
C
.

Elements of the bounds.

Here we define the quantities that we use to state the bounds on the NoBLE coefficients.
In the following, we define the vectors
\vec{P},\vec{P}^{\iota},\vec{E},\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}},\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{end}},\vec{\Delta}^{\iota}\in\Rbold^{5}

and the matrices
{\bf{\bar{A}}},{\bf A},{\bf C}\in\Rbold^{5\times 5}
by their entries. For convenience, we use
\{-2,-1,0,1,2\}
as indices for the entries of the vectors and matrices.
Let

\displaystyle(\vec{P})_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,y}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}(x,y),\qquad\qquad(\vec{P}^{\iota})_{m}=\sum_{x,y}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,m}(x,y),

(5.1)

\displaystyle(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})_{m}

\displaystyle=\sup_{y}\sum_{x}\Delta^{\rm{start},m}(x,x+y),\quad(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{end}})_{m}=\sup_{y}\sum_{x}\Delta^{\rm{start},m}(x,x+y),

(5.2)

\displaystyle(\vec{\Delta}^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle I}})_{m}

\displaystyle=\sup_{y}\sum_{x}\Delta^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,x+y),\qquad(\vec{\Delta}^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle II}})_{m}=\sup_{y}\sum_{x}\Delta^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,x+y),

(5.3)

and

\displaystyle({\bf A})_{l,m}

\displaystyle=\sup_{v}\sum_{x,y}A^{l,m}(0,v,x,y),\qquad\qquad({\bf C})_{l,m}=\sup_{v,y}\sum_{x}C^{l,m}(0,v,x,x+y),

(5.4)

\displaystyle(\vec{E}^{\rm{open}})_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v,x}A^{m,0}(x,x,v,0),\qquad\qquad(\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}})_{m}=\sum_{v,x}A^{0,m}(x,x,v,0).

(5.5)

Using the bootstrap assumption
f_{i}(z)<\Gamma_{i}
we can compute explicit numerical bound for each of these quantities. We use these quantities to formulate the bounds on the NoBLE coefficients.

5.2
The bounds

Now we state the required bound on the coefficients. For this we use the notation
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}
, as used in (
1.19
), where
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}
is
1
in bounds for LAs and
0
in bounds for LTs. We have to be especially careful in our bounds for
N=0
and
N=1
, which turn out to be the major contributions to the NoBLE coefficients. Therefore, we distinguish between the bounds for
N=0
,
N=1
and
N\geq 2
. We start by formulating the bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}
and
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}
:

Lemma 5.1

(Bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}
and
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}
)
.

Let
0\leq z<z_{c}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{x}\mathscr{D}_{1}(x),\qquad\quad\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)\leq\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{2}(x),

(5.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2d-2}{2d}\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{2}(x,0),

(5.7)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{2}\sum_{x}|x|^{2}G_{z}(x)2dz(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x),

(5.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{2}\sum_{x}|x|^{2}G_{z}(x)(2dz)^{2}g_{z}(D^{\star 2}\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x),

(5.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2d-2}{2d}\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{2}\sum_{x}|x|^{2}G_{z}(x)(2dz)^{2}g_{z}(D^{\star 2}\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x).

(5.10)

Further,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}^{\iota}}\big((2d-1)\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},3}({e}_{1},0)+\frac{2d-2}{2d}\sum_{x}\mathscr{B}_{2,2}(x,0)\big),

(5.11)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle 4d{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},3}({e}_{1},0)

(5.12)

\displaystyle+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{2}\sum_{x}(1+|x|^{2})(2dz)^{2}g_{z}(D^{\star 2}\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)G_{z}(x).

We continue by formulating the bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}
and
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}
. To state the bound on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}
and the remainder of the split
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}
and
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
, we use the abbreviations
\alpha_{n,m}
,
\beta_{n}
and
\gamma_{n}
, that will only be used in this lemma, and which, for
n,m\in\{0,1,2\}
, are given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{n,m}=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})\sum_{x}\mathscr{D}_{n}(x)+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m=0\}}\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota})+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{x}\mathscr{T}_{1,\max\{1,m\},n}({e}_{\iota},x,0)

(5.13)

\displaystyle+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\big({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{n\leq 1\}}\sup_{y}\tilde{G}_{2,z}(y)(2d-1)zg_{z}\tilde{G}_{3,z}({e}_{\iota})+\sup_{y}\tilde{G}_{m,z}(y)\sum_{x\neq 0}\mathscr{D}_{2}(x)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\sup_{u}\sum_{x\neq 0}\mathscr{B}_{n,1}(x,u)\big),

\displaystyle\beta_{n}=

\displaystyle 2dz\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)\big(\frac{1}{2}(D\star\tilde{G}_{n,z})(x)+(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x-{e}_{\iota})\big)

(5.14)

\displaystyle+2dz\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sup_{y}\tilde{G}_{1,z}(y)\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)\big(\frac{1}{2}(D\star\tilde{G}_{1,z})(x)+(D\star\tilde{G}_{z}\star\tilde{G}_{1,z})(x)\big),

\displaystyle\gamma_{n}=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})\sum_{x}\big(\mathscr{D}_{1}(x)+|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)dz(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)\big)

(5.15)

\displaystyle+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\big(\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})+\sup_{y}G_{n,z}(y)\big)\sum_{x}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\bar{G}(x-{e}_{\iota})2dz(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)

\displaystyle+2\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\sup_{u}\sum_{x}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(x,u)

\displaystyle+4dz\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sup_{y}\tilde{G}_{1,z}(y)\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)(D\star\tilde{G}_{z}\star\tilde{G}_{1,z})(x).

Then the bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}
and
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}
read as follows:

Lemma 5.2

(Bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}
and
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}
)
.

Let
0\leq z<z_{c}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle G_{z}({e}_{\iota})+\alpha_{1,0},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\beta_{0},

(5.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle G_{z}({e}_{\iota})+\gamma_{1}.

(5.18)

The remainder terms of the split are bounded in a similar way as

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{1,2},\qquad\qquad\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)\leq\gamma_{2},

(5.19)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{2,1},\qquad\qquad\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)\leq\beta_{1}.

(5.20)

The explicit terms of the splits are bounded by

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{\iota})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota}),\quad\qquad\qquad\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}(0)\leq G_{z}({e}_{\iota}),

(5.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota})\leq

\displaystyle G_{z}({e}_{\iota})+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{\kappa\colon\kappa\neq-\iota}\mathscr{T}_{1,\underline{1},2}({e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota},0)+\frac{zg_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\sum_{\kappa\colon\kappa\neq-\iota}\mathscr{B}_{2,2}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa},0),

(5.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}({e}_{\kappa})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota})+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}zg_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}\sum_{\kappa\colon\kappa\neq-\iota}\sup_{x\neq 0}\tilde{G}_{1,z}(x)\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\kappa}),

(5.23)

\displaystyle+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\sum_{\kappa\colon\kappa\neq-\iota}\Big(\frac{1}{g_{z}}\mathscr{T}_{1,2,\underline{1}}({e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa},0)+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}zG_{3,z}({e}_{\kappa})\big(\tilde{G}_{1,z}({e}_{\iota})+\tilde{G}_{2,z}({e}_{\kappa}-{e}_{\iota})\big)\Big).

We bound the terms in the split of
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}
as

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa}\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{\alpha,z}({e}_{\iota})\leq

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}(2d-1)z\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota}),

(5.24)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{\iota}\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle(2d-1)zg_{z}G_{z}({e}_{\iota})+zg_{z}\alpha_{1,1},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\sum_{x,\iota,\kappa}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x+{e}_{\kappa})\leq

\displaystyle 2(2d)^{2}zg_{z}G_{z}({e}_{\iota})+(2d)^{2}zg_{z}\gamma_{1}+4d(d+1)zg_{z}\alpha_{1,1}.

(5.26)

Lemma 5.3

(Bounds on the coefficients for
N=1
)
.

Let
0\leq z<z_{c}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}(\vec{P})_{0},

(5.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}\Big((\vec{P})_{0}-\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}A^{0,0}(0,0,x,x)+2\mathscr{B}_{2,2}(x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}(x,w,0)\Big),

(5.28)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}\Big((\vec{P})_{0}-\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}A^{0,0}(0,0,x,x)+2{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{{e}_{\kappa}\neq x\}}\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},3}(x,0)\Big)

(5.29)

\displaystyle+\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(2\mathscr{B}_{2,2}(x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{1,1,1}(x,w,0)\Big),

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}\Big((\vec{P})_{0}-\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}A^{0,0}(0,0,x,x)+2\mathscr{B}_{2,2}(x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}(x,w,0)\Big),

(5.30)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})_{0},\qquad\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)\leq(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})_{0},

(5.31)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})_{0},\qquad\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)\leq(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})_{0}+\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x).

(5.32)

Further,
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g_{z}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}(\vec{P}^{\iota})_{0},

(5.33)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle(\vec{\Delta}^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle I}})_{0},

(5.34)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle(\vec{\Delta}^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle II}})_{0}.

(5.35)

Proposition 5.4

(Bounds on the coefficients for
N\geq 2
)
.

Let
0\leq z<z_{c}
and
N\geq 2
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g_{z}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}\vec{P}^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}{\vec{E}}^{\rm{open}},\quad\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq\frac{g_{z}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}(\vec{P}^{\iota})^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}{\vec{E}}^{\rm{open}},

(5.36)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\ N\left((\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}{\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}}}+\vec{P}^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{end}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+N{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{N>2\}}\sum_{M=0}^{N-3}\vec{P}{\bf A}^{M}{\bf C}{\bf A}^{N-3-M}\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}},

(5.37)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\ N\left((\vec{\Delta}^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle I}})^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}{\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}}}+(\vec{P}^{\iota})^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{end}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+N{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{N>2\}}\sum_{M=0}^{N-3}\vec{P}^{\iota}{\bf A}^{M}{\bf C}{\bf A}^{N-3-M}\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}},

(5.38)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\ N\left((\vec{\Delta}^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle II}})^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}{\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}}}+(\vec{P}^{\iota})^{T}{\bf A}^{N-2}\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{end}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+N{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{N>2\}}\sum_{M=0}^{N-3}\vec{P}^{\iota}{\bf A}^{M}{\bf C}{\bf A}^{N-3-M}\vec{E}^{\rm{closed}}.

(5.39)

N-2

Figure 16
:
Schematic representation of the bound on
\hat{\Xi}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(0)
, given in (
5.36
). The backbone is marked by a thicker line.
See also Figures
10
and
11
.

Having stated our main bounds on the NoBLE coefficients, we next state some additional bounds that improve the numerical accuracy of our method:

Lemma 5.5

(Bounds on differences)
.

Let
0\leq z<z_{c}
. Then,

\displaystyle\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}_{\alpha,z}(0)-\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}_{\alpha,z}(0)\leq

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\qquad\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}_{\alpha,z}({e}_{1})-\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}_{\alpha,z}({e}_{1})\leq 0,

(5.40)

\displaystyle\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}_{\alpha,z}(0)-\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}_{\alpha,z}(0)\leq

\displaystyle 2dzg^{\iota}_{z}G_{3,z}({e}_{\iota}),

(5.41)

\displaystyle\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}_{\alpha,z}({e}_{1})-\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}_{\alpha,z}({e}_{1})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}\Big(2\mathscr{B}_{3,\underline{1}}({e}_{1},0)+\sum_{v}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,1}({e}_{1},v,0)\Big),

(5.42)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota}\big(\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{1}+{e}_{\iota})-\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{1}+{e}_{\iota})\big)\leq\sum_{\iota}\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{0,0,\underline{2}}(w,{e}_{1}+{e}_{\iota},0),

(5.43)

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota}\big(\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{1}+{e}_{\iota})-\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{1}+{e}_{\iota})\big)\leq{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}\sum_{\iota}\mathscr{B}_{2,\underline{2}}({e}_{1}+{e}_{\iota},0).

(5.44)

Further,

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota}\big(\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}

\displaystyle({e}_{\iota})-\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}({e}_{\iota})\big)\leq(2d-1)\Big(2\mathscr{B}_{3,\underline{1}}({e}_{1},0)+\sum_{v}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,1}({e}_{1},v,0)\Big),

(5.45)

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota}\big(\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}

\displaystyle({e}_{\iota})-\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},1}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}({e}_{\iota})\big)\leq 0.

(5.46)

In the following, we discuss some bounds that are not stated in Table
1
. These are also required for the analysis, but are in general very easy to obtain.
We start by bounding
\bar{\mu}_{z}/\mu_{z}
, as formulated in
[
12
, first inequality in (4.30) in Assumption 4.3]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\mu}_{z}}{\mu_{z}}=\frac{zg_{z}}{zg_{z}^{\iota}}=1+\frac{g_{z}-g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}^{\iota}}=1+\frac{\bar{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})}{g_{z}^{\iota}}\leq 1+2dz(D\star\bar{G}_{z})({e}_{\iota}):=\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle\mu}.

Regarding
\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle\mu}
, we remark that by the definition of
z_{I}
in (
3.55
), for each
z>z_{I}
,

\displaystyle zg_{z}\geq{z_{I}}g_{z_{I}}=\frac{1}{(2d-1){\mathrm{e}}}:=\underline{\beta}_{\mu},

as formulated in
[
12
, second inequality in (4.30) in Assumption 4.3]
.

[
12
, Assumption 4.3]
also states lower bonds on
\Psi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}
and
\Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}
, which we could obtain using simple combinatorics. We numerically found that the benefit of these extra bounds is minimal, so that we simply use the trivial lower
\underline{\beta}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}=\underline{\beta}_{\scriptscriptstyle\sum\Pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}=\underline{\beta}_{\scriptscriptstyle\sum\Pi_{\alpha}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}=0
for these coefficients, which are by definition non-negative.

Further, we assume that the geometric series bounding our NoBLE coefficients converge, which follows if their base is smaller than one as formulated in
[
12
, (4.34) in Assumption 4.3]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{(2d-1)\bar{\mu}_{z}}{1-\mu_{z}}\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\beta_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Xi}^{\iota}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}<1.

(5.47)

For example, this shows that the matrix inverse in (
2.12
) is well defined. Finally, the inequalities
\underline{\beta}_{\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,F}-\underline{\beta}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Delta R,F}>0
and
\underline{\beta}_{\scriptscriptstyle c,\Phi}-\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle|\alpha,\Phi|}-\beta_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,\Phi}}>0
, which guarantees that the numerator and denominator of
\hat{G}_{z}(k)
in (
2.12
) are non-negative, see
[
12
, one-but-last sentence of Assumption 2.7]
.
These are numerical conditions that are verified explicitly in the Mathematica notebooks. These conditions are relatively weak, in the sense that empirically, we observe that the bootstrap analysis, which in particular includes an improvement of bounds, is much more likely to fail.

6
Proof of the bounds on the NoBLE coefficients

In this section, we explain how the bounds stated in the preceding section are proven. In Section
4
, we have already explained the basic idea of the bounds. We start by proving the bounds on the relatively simple coefficients arising for
N=0
and
N=1
. Then, we explain how the coefficients for general
N\geq 2
are bounded. After this, we discuss bounds on the difference of coefficients.

6.1
Proof of bounds for
N=0

Here we prove Lemma
5.1
and
5.2
.
For
N=0
, the coefficients are created by LAs in which
0
and
x
are doubly connected, see (
3.24
). In Section

4.4
we have explained how to bound the contribution of such LAs, so we add here only the missing details.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

In Section
4.4
we have already proven the bound on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{z}
. From
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}
, we have extracted the contributions in which
0
and
x
are connected by a direct bond, see (
3.36
). Considering this, the bounds (
5.6
) and (
5.9
) follow directly.
The only difference between
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}
and
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
is that
x-{e}_{\kappa}\not\in A
for
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
.
Since each double connection requires at least two neighbors of
x
to be part of
A
, the spatial symmetry of the LAs implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)=\frac{1}{2d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\kappa}\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)\leq\frac{2d-2}{2d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x),

(6.1)

which implies (
5.7
) and (
5.10
).

The coefficient
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)
was created by extracting the shortest contributions with
x={e}_{\kappa}
and
x
’s that are neighbors of
{e}_{\kappa}
. The contribution of the remaining
(2d-1)
neighbors of the origin is bounded using
\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},3}({e}_{1},0)
. For all other
x
, i.e., those with
|x|>1
, we use
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)\leq\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)
to conclude the bounds stated in (
5.11
).

In (
5.12
), we bound the contribution due to all neighbors of origin by the first term.
The contribution due to all other
x
are bounded by the second term. Regarding the neighbors
x={e}_{\iota}
of the origin, with
\iota\in\{\pm 1,\dots,\pm d\}
, we note that

\displaystyle|x-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}=|{e}_{\iota}-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}=\begin{cases}2&\text{ for~}|\iota|\neq|\kappa|,\\
4&\text{ for }\iota=-\kappa,\\
0&\text{ for }\iota=\kappa.\end{cases}

(6.2)

Summing over
\iota
creates the stated factor
4d
.
For the other
x
, we know that any path to
x
contains at least two bonds.
To be able to use a symmetry argument, we ignore that we extracted terms and as well as the condition
x-{e}_{\kappa}\not\in A
, to obtain the simple bound

\displaystyle(2dz)^{2}g_{z}(D^{\star 2}\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)\bar{G}_{z}(x)

(6.3)

Then, we use
|x-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}=(|x|^{2}-2x_{\kappa}+1)
and that the term involving
-2x_{\kappa}
sums to zero by symmetry, to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{x}|x-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}(2dz)^{2}g_{z}(D^{\star 2}\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)\bar{G}_{z}(x)=\sum_{x}(|x|^{2}+1)(2dz)^{2}g_{z}(D^{\star 2}\star\tilde{G}_{z})(x)\bar{G}_{z}(x),

(6.4)

which is the second term in (
5.12
).
This proves (
5.12
), and completes the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

By definition,

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)=\frac{1}{g_{z}}\sum_{A\colon 0,x,{e}_{\iota}\in A}z^{|A|}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}.

(6.5)

For LTs, this simplifies to

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)=\frac{\delta_{0,x}}{g_{z}}\bar{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})=\delta_{0,x}G_{z}({e}_{\iota}).

(6.6)

For LAs we recall that we have bounded such animals already between (
4.14
) and (
4.15
).

We improve these bounds by using
repulsive diagrams
(recall Section
4.3
) and by considering four cases for the point
u
, where
u
is the summand appearing in (
4.14
), thus yielding the bound
\alpha_{1,1}
(see (
5.13
)). Let us explain the four cases for
u
:

(a) For
u=0
, the diagram is given by a double connection from
0
to
x
and a simple path from
0
to
{e}_{\iota}
that we bound by

\displaystyle G_{z}({e}_{\iota})+\frac{1}{g_{z}}\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})\sum_{x\neq 0}\mathscr{D}_{1}(x,0).

(6.7)

The first term is given explicitly in (
5.16
) and the second term corresponds to the first term of
\alpha_{1,1}
.

(b) For
u={e}_{\iota}
, the point
{e}_{\iota}
is on the double connection between
0
and
x
.
For
x={e}_{\iota}
, we have a simple double connection to
{e}_{\iota}
, otherwise a repulsive triangle, as stated in the second and third term of
\alpha_{1,1}
.

(c) In the contributions in which
u=x\not\in\{0,{e}_{\iota}\}
we find a double connection between
0
and
x
, bounded by
\mathscr{D}_{1}(x,0)
and a connection from
x
to
{e}_{\iota}
, bounded by
\tilde{G}_{m,z}(x-{e}_{\iota})
. We improve this bound slightly using the parity of the lattice:
If
0
and
x
are directly connected, the length of the connection between
x
to
{e}_{\iota}
is at least
2
.

(d) In the remaining contributions, we have that
u\neq\{0,x,{e}_{\iota}\}
, so that these LAs can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\sup_{u}\sum_{x}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(x,u),

(6.8)

which corresponds to the last term of
\alpha_{1,1}
. This completes the proof of (
5.16
).

The diagrams with the additional weights
|x|^{2}
and
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
are bounded in a similar manner. As the numerical bounds, using the bootstrap function
f_{3}
, require a
G_{z}
to carry the spatial weight, we express the bounds using two-point functions instead of repulsive diagrams. For the weight
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
, two additional steps are made: Firstly, for
u=0\neq x
we first split the weights using symmetry, as shown in (
6.4
), and then bound the two arising terms. This leads to the first line of
\gamma_{1}
in (
5.15
). Secondly, for
u\neq\{0,{e}_{\iota},x\}
, we use that
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\leq 2(|x|^{2}+1)
, see
[
12
, (2.24)]
, and bound the arising terms individually. This completes the proofs of (
5.17
) and (
5.18
).

For the bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}
and
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
stated in (
5.19
)-(
5.20
), we review which terms have been extracted from
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
to yield
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}
and
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
. For
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)
, the LAs in which
x
is directly connected to the origin have been removed. For this reason, in
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
all paths from
0
to
x
have a length of at least
2
instead of
1
. This is expressed by the subscripts
n
of
\alpha_{n,m}
and
\beta_{n}
. To obtain
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)
from
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
, we have extracted all LAs in which
x
is directly connected to
{e}_{\iota}
. This means that in
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x)
, the path from
{e}_{\iota}
to
x
in contributing LAs have length at least
2
.
The bounds (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) are created by repeating the consideration of cases for
u
, as given above for the bound on bound
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
, while keeping these additional restrictions.

We continue with the bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}
and
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}
in (
5.21
)–(
5.23
). Note that for LTs, most of these terms are zero due to the lack of double connections, so we restrict to LAs. Recall (
3.34
), as well as (
3.50
)–(
3.51
).

By their definitions in (
3.39
) and (
3.40
),

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{\iota})=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{A\colon 0,{e}_{\iota}\in A}z^{|A|}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}{e}_{\iota}\}}\leq\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota}),

(6.9)

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}(0)=

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}(0)=\frac{1}{g_{z}}\bar{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})=G_{z}({e}_{\iota}),

(6.10)

which proves (
5.21
). To obtain the bound in (
5.22
), we again use (
3.39
), and remark that, for
x\neq 0
with
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}=1
,
we can write
x={e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota}
with
\kappa\neq-\iota
to get

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g_{z}}\sum_{A\colon 0,{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota},{e}_{\iota}\in A}z^{|A|}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota}\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{d_{A}({e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota})=1\}}.

(6.11)

Thus,
{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota}
and
{e}_{\iota}
are directly connected in the LA. We distinguish between the case where
{e}_{\iota}
is on the double connection or not to obtain

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}({e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{T}_{1,\underline{1},2}({e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota},0)+\frac{zg_{z}^{\iota}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}(1-\delta_{\kappa,-\iota})\mathscr{B}_{2,2}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa},0).

(6.12)

Summing over all neighbors
{e}_{\kappa}+{e}_{\iota}
of
{e}_{\iota}
, and also using that the contribution of
\kappa=-\iota
is
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle I},z}(0)=G_{z}({e}_{\iota})
, proves (
5.22
).

For
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}({e}_{\kappa})
, we recall (
3.40
), and consider
\kappa\neq\iota
, distinguish between the cases where
{e}_{\iota}
is part of the double connection from
0
to
{e}_{\kappa}
or not. For this, let
u
be the first point that any path between
0
and
{e}_{\iota}
, and the path between
{e}_{\kappa}
and
{e}_{\iota}
, share.
We split between
u=0,{e}_{\kappa},{e}_{\iota}
and
u\not\in\{0,{e}_{\kappa},{e}_{\iota}\}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{\alpha,{\scriptscriptstyle II},z}({e}_{\kappa})=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\sum_{A\colon 0,{e}_{\kappa},{e}_{\iota}\in A}z^{|A|}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}{e}_{\kappa}\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{d_{A}(0,{e}_{\kappa})=1\}}

(6.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle z{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}G_{3,z}({e}_{\kappa})\big(\tilde{G}_{1,z}({e}_{\iota})+\tilde{G}_{2,z}({e}_{\kappa}-{e}_{\iota})\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{T}_{1,2,\underline{1}}({e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa},0)+z\frac{g_{z}^{\iota}}{g_{z}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\sup_{x\neq 0}\tilde{G}_{1,z}(x)\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\kappa}).

Summing this over all neighbors
{e}_{\kappa}
of the origin, and using that the contribution for
\kappa=\iota
can be bounded by
\frac{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota})
, proves (
5.23
).

For the bounds on
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z},\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{\alpha,z}
and
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}
stated in (
5.24
)–(
5.26
), it is useful to write out the definition of
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}
in (
3.28
), and simplify it for the special case
N=0
. This leads to

\displaystyle\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{A\colon 0,x\in A}z^{|A|+1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}x\}}\big({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{{e}_{\iota}\in A_{0}^{\omega}\}}+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{|\omega|=0\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{x\neq 0\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{x-{e}_{\kappa}={e}_{\iota}\}}\big){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{x-{e}_{\kappa}\not\in A\}}.

(6.14)

Using the split
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{\alpha,z}
(see (
3.41
)), we extract from this the contributions where
x={e}_{\iota}
, as well as those with

x={e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa}
in which the edge
({e}_{\iota},{e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa})
is in the LA. For
x={e}_{\iota}
, we see that

\displaystyle\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{\alpha,z}({e}_{\iota})

\displaystyle=\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}({e}_{\iota})={\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\sum_{A\colon 0,{e}_{\iota}\in A}z^{|A|+1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\Longleftrightarrow}}{e}_{\iota}\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{{e}_{\iota}-{e}_{\kappa}\not\in A\}}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}(1-\delta_{\iota,-\kappa})z\mathscr{D}_{1}({e}_{\iota}),

(6.15)

as the LAs contain a simple double connection to a neighbor of the origin. Summing this over
\kappa
proves (
5.24
).
The bounds (
5.25
) and (
5.26
) basically follow from the fact that
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}(x)\leq zg_{z}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
, see (
3.72
).
To understand (
5.25
) we compare the bound with (
5.16
).
When summing over
\iota
for
x=0
,
\iota=-\kappa
does not contribute explaining the factor
(2d-1)
.
Further, we have extracted the case that
x={e}_{\iota}
, thus we can us
\alpha_{1,1}
instead of
\alpha_{1,0}
for this bound.

For (
5.26
), we need to redistribute the weights. We write

\displaystyle\sum_{x,\iota,\kappa}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x+{e}_{\kappa})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\iota,\kappa}\sum_{x\neq{e}_{\iota}}|x-{e}_{\iota}-{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)

(6.16)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\iota,\kappa}|{e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa}|^{2}\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}zg_{z}\sum_{\iota,\kappa}\sum_{x\not\in\{0,{e}_{\iota}\}}[|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}+1-2(x_{\kappa}-\delta_{\iota,\kappa})]\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x),

since
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}(x)\leq zg_{z}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
. The first contribution is bounded by
2(2d)^{2}zg_{z}G_{z}({e}_{\iota})
. We use (
5.18
)
for the
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
contribution.
We further use (
5.16
) (without the term created by
x\in\{0,{e}_{\iota}\}
) to obtain a bound
(2d)^{2}zg_{z}\alpha_{1,1}
on the contribution due to
+1
. Finally,
\sum_{\iota}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
is symmetric, so that the
x_{\kappa}
contribution vanishes, while the
\delta_{\iota,\kappa}
contribution gives
2\sum_{\iota,x\not\in\{0,{e}_{\iota}\}}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 0})},\iota}_{z}(x)
, which can be taken together with the
+1
contribution using (
5.16
).
∎

6.2

, stated in

The first step to obtain the bounds are the following
x

Lemma 6.1

(Pointwise bounds)
.

For all
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\iota
and

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g_{z}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},0}_{z}(x,x),

(6.17)

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g_{z}}{g^{\iota}_{z}}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,0}_{z}(x,x).

(6.18)

We define
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},0}
in Table

.
The definition of

.

\underline{b}
Terms of

Table 2
:

Proof.

The coefficients that we consider here are defined by sausage-walks for which only the first and last sausage intersect. In Section
4.1
, we have discussed how to bound
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)
for LTs and later obtained the bound (
4.16
) for LAs. Using that all connections are repulsive, we can improve (
4.16
) to

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g^{\iota}_{z}}{g_{z}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}(x,w,0)+\frac{1}{g_{z}}\sum_{u,w,\underline{b}}\mathscr{S}_{1,0,0,0}(x-\underline{b},w-\underline{b},u-\underline{b},0)\mathscr{B}_{1,0}(-\underline{b},w-\underline{b}).

As discussed in the third improvement in Section
4.1
, each loop in this diagram consists of at least four
bonds, and we have that
w\not\in b
. All this is implied by the non-backtracking condition in the NoBLE.
We make use of this by considering some special cases for
b
and
u
, as summarized in Table
2
,
which improves the bound to obtain (
5.27
):

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g^{\iota}_{z}}{g_{z}}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},0}_{z}(x,x).

(6.19)

Bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}
.

For
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}
we know that either
\overline{b}_{1}={e}_{\iota}
or
{e}_{\iota}\in A_{0}^{\omega}
as enforced by the indicator
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\iota}(\omega)
defined in (
3.16
).
To create a bound we consider
16
possible forms of the diagram and then bound each of them separately. Then, we define
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,0}_{z}(x,x)
, as well as
\Delta^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle I},0}
and
\Delta^{\iota,{\scriptscriptstyle II},0}
, as the sum of these
16
individual diagrams.
The necessary explanation and definitions of these diagrams are presented in Appendices
C.2.2
-
C.4.2

of this extended version.

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.1
.
∎

We continue with the proof of Lemma
5.3
:

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

The bounds in (
5.27
) and (
5.33
) follow by summing the pointwise bounds in Lemma
6.1
over
x
.

Weighted diagrams for
\Xi_{z}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}(x)
.

Now we derive a bound for the weighted diagram
|x|^{2}\Xi_{z}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}(x)
.
Below (
4.16
), we have argued that we can split the sausage-walk such that (only) one line is bounded by
\bar{G}_{z}
.
Multiplying such a bound with
|x|^{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\bar{\Xi}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{w,x}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)\tilde{G}_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(w-x)

(6.20)

\displaystyle+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\sum_{x,w,u}\sum_{\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(u-\underline{b}_{1})2dzg^{\iota}_{z}(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\tilde{G}_{z}(u-w)\bar{G}_{z}(\underline{b}_{1})\tilde{G}_{z}(w-x)\tilde{G}_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1}).

(6.21)

For the second term, which is not present for LTs, we have the problem
that there is no single line connecting
0
and
x
to which we allocate the weight
|x|^{2}
. This is problematic as the bootstrap function
f_{3}
only allows us to bound
|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)
.
For this reason, we go back to an earlier step in the bounding procedure.
We first apply
|x|^{2}\leq 2|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+2|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}
when still summing over the sausage-walks. Only, then we split the sausages and chose which line is bounded by
\bar{G}_{z}
. This creates the bound

\displaystyle\eqref{e:BoundLT-tmp-2.5}\leq

\displaystyle 2{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}^{a}\sum_{u,w,x}\sum_{\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0}(2dzg^{\iota}_{z})(D\star\tilde{G}_{z})(u)\tilde{G}_{z}(u-\underline{b}_{1})\tilde{G}_{z}(x-w)\tilde{G}_{z}(w-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(\underline{b}_{1})\tilde{G}_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})+\tilde{G}_{z}(\underline{b}_{1})|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})\big)

(6.22)

This term can be bounded using our bootstrap functions.
We improve this bound using three ideas, that we now explain.
To improve the first line (
6.20
), we consider the cases
w=0
,
w=x
and
w\not\in\{0,x\}
to obtain the bound

\displaystyle\sum_{w,x}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)\tilde{G}_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(w-x)\leq\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(x)\left(4dz(D\star G_{z})(x)+(2dz)^{2}(D^{\star 2}\star G^{\star 2}_{z})(x)\right).

To improve (
6.22
) we use a total of five special cases for
u,w
and
\underline{b}_{1}
and will omit the details here.

The third idea is to use symmetry, of which we provide an example when
u=\underline{b}_{1}
and
x=y+u
. In this case, we split the weight using
|u+y|^{2}=|y|^{2}+|u|^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}y_{i}
and conclude a bound of the form

\displaystyle\sum_{u,y,w}\left(|u|^{2}+|y|^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}y_{i}\right)\bar{G}_{z}(u)\tilde{G}_{z}(u)\bar{G}_{z}(y)\tilde{G}_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{u}|u|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(u)\tilde{G}_{z}(u)\sum_{y,w}\bar{G}_{z}(y)\tilde{G}_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{u}\bar{G}_{z}(u)\tilde{G}_{z}(u)\sum_{y,w}|y|^{2}\bar{G}_{z}(y)\tilde{G}_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w).

(6.23)

The mixed weights
u_{i}y_{i}
cancel since the bubble to
u
, as well as the triangle to
y
, are symmetric in each coordinate. This saves us a factor
2
, for
u=\underline{b}_{1}
when compared to (
6.22
). The building block
(\vec{\Delta}^{\rm{start}})_{0}
is defined as the sum of these terms. This proves (
5.31
).

Bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z},\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z},\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}
.

These coefficients have been obtained by extracting some explicit contributions from
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}
resulting in very similar bounds as for
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}
. The diagrammatic representation of these coefficients are all similar to
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},0}_{z}
depicted in Figure
15
. In all cases we have extracted contributions to the left square of Figure
15
, which is bounded by
A^{0,0}
.
We derive the bounds in (
5.28
), (
5.29
) and (
5.30
) by removing the contribution
A^{0,0}
, and then add the parts of this square that are actually required.

For
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}
and
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}
, we have extracted the case
x=0
, as well as all contributions in which
0
and
x
are directly connection via a bond, see (
3.42
). For this reason, all connections from
0
to
x
consist of at least
2
steps, which is reflected in (
5.28
) and (
5.30
).

For
\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}
, we have only extracted the contribution of
x=0
and
x={e}_{\kappa}
.
Thus, when comparing (
5.29
) and (
5.30
), we see that we have to add to
(
5.29
) also the contribution of points
x\neq{e}_{\kappa}
that can be reached within one step.

Regarding the weighted bounds stated in (
5.31
)-(
5.32
), it (numerically) turns out that we cannot benefit from the fact that we have extracted terms. For this reason we simply note that
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R},z}(x)\leq\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)
and
\max\{\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,I},z}(x),\Psi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle R,II},z}(x)\}\leq\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}}_{z}(x)
,
and apply the original bounds for these terms.

Weighted bounds on
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}
.

The bounds on the weighted diagrams of
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota}_{z}
in (
5.34
)–(
5.35
) follow by carefully analysing the weighted version of
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,0}_{z}(x,x)
, which appear as sums of
32
individual diagrams.
The necessary explanation and definitions of these diagrams are again presented in Appendix
C.4.2

of this extended version.

The above bounds prove Lemma
5.3
.
∎

6.3

In this section we will explain how we bound the NoBLE coefficients with

Decomposition of weights.

We bound the diagram for

\displaystyle\big|\sum_{i=1}^{J}x_{i}\big|^{2}=

\displaystyle\big|\sum_{i=1}^{J}x_{i}\big|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle J\sum_{i=1}^{J}|x_{i}|^{2}.

(6.24)

. The wide line represents the path of the backbone.

After applying (

individually. This will be done in (

,

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x,\Delta_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{g_{z}}\sum_{\omega\in\mathcal{W}(x)}z^{|\omega|}Z[0,|\omega|]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}[0,|\omega|]}\prod_{st\in L}(-\mathcal{U}_{st})\prod_{s^{\prime}t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}(L)}(1+\mathcal{U}_{s^{\prime}t^{\prime}})|x_{i}(\omega,L)|^{2}

(6.25)

with
|x_{i}(\omega,L)|^{2}
shown in Figure
17
.
We define
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x,\Delta_{i})
accordingly with the additional indicator
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\iota}(\omega)

(compare (
3.25
) and (
3.26
)). It is possible to give the formal definition of
x_{i}(\omega,L)
using the lace
L={s_{1}t_{1},\dots,s_{N}t_{N}}
, the backbone
(b^{\omega}_{j})_{j=1}^{|\omega|}
, and the sausages that are being split
S_{s_{i}}^{\omega}
,
S_{t_{i}}^{\omega}
. As this would not be very informative, we omit this and just refer to Figure
17
instead. Figure
17
allows for a straightforward adaptation for all
N\geq 3
. Then we obtain, using (
6.24
),

\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x)\leq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x,\Delta_{i}).

(6.26)

Construction of the bounding diagram.

We combine the building blocks, see Section
5.1
, to construct the bounds on the NoBLE coefficients: We recursively define

\displaystyle P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},m}(x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{l=-2}^{2}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(u,v)A^{l,m}(u,v,x,y),

(6.27)

\displaystyle P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota,m}(x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{l=-2}^{2}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},\iota,l}(u,v)A^{l,m}(u,v,x,y),

(6.28)

\displaystyle Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}(t,y;x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A^{0,m}(x,x,t,y),

(6.29)

\displaystyle Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},m}(t,y;x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{l=-2}^{2}Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(u,v;x)A^{l,m}(u,v,t,y),

(6.30)

for
N\geq 2
and
m\in\{-2,-1,\dots,2\}
.
The formal definition of
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}
and
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},\iota,m}
, which serve as the initialisation of
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},m}
and
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota,m}
for
N=1
is given in Appendix
C.2.1
and
C.2.2
.

Using these constructs we bound the coefficients as follows:

Lemma 6.2

(Pointwise bounds)
.

For all
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\iota
and
0\leq z\leq z_{c}
, and
N\geq 2

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g^{\iota}_{z}}{g_{z}}\sum_{u,v,l}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(u,v)A^{l,0}(u,v,x,x),

(6.31)

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x,\Delta_{N})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v,l}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(u,v)\Delta^{{\rm end},l}(u,v,x,x),

(6.32)

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}_{z}(x,\Delta_{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v,l}\Delta^{{\rm start},l}(x_{1},v)Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(u,v;x).

(6.33)

Further, for
N,M\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N+M+1})}}_{z}(x,\Delta_{N+1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v,w,y}\sum_{l,m}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},l}(u,w)C^{l,m}(u,v,w,y)Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},m}(w,y;x).

(6.34)

The same bounds hold for
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}
, when
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}
and
\Delta^{{\rm start},l}

are replaced by
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},\iota,l}
and
\Delta^{{\rm iota},{\scriptscriptstyle I},l}
or
\Delta^{{\rm iota},{\scriptscriptstyle II},l}
, respectively.

Combining all these bounds we are able to bound the weighted diagrams. First, we split the weight
|x|^{2}
using
(
6.24
), as formulated in (
6.26
). Then, we use the bounds in Lemma
6.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}(x)\leq

\displaystyle N\sum_{l,x_{1},v,x}\Delta^{{\rm start},l}(x_{1},v)Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(x_{1},v;x)

\displaystyle+N\sum_{M=1}^{N-2}\sum_{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}l,m,u,\\
v,w,x,y\end{array}}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},l}(u,v)C^{l,m}(u,v,w,y)Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-M-1})},m}(w,y;x)

\displaystyle+N\sum_{l,x_{N},v,x}P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N-1})},l}(x-x_{N},x+v)\Delta^{{\rm end},l}(0,v,x_{N},x_{N}),

(6.37)

which implies the bound stated in (
5.37
).

Let us highlight once more how crucial the order is in which we create our bounds:
First, we group each combination
(\omega,L)
according to the length and role of shared lines
(l_{i})_{i=1,\dots,N-1}
.
Secondly, we identify the first intersection points at which we split the sausage-walks.
Thirdly, we use (
6.24
) to split the weight along the bottom lines, as shown in
Figure
17
.
In the fourth step, we apply, depending on the weight
|x_{i}|^{2}
, one of the bounds of Lemma
6.2
.
Then we also allocate the one-point functions such that the weight is along a line corresponding to a two-point function
G_{z}
.

w_{1}
0
w_{2}
x
u
\underline{b}^{\omega}_{s_{2}}=\underline{b}^{\omega}_{t_{1}}=y
=-m
w_{1}
0
x
w_{2}
\underline{b}^{\omega}_{t_{1}}=u
\underline{b}^{\omega}_{s_{2}}=y
=m

Figure 18
:
Labeling in the proof of the bound on
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})}_{z}
for
\underline{b}_{0}=0
.
The left diagram corresponds to
s_{2}=t_{1}
and the right to
s_{2}<t_{1}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

We divide the proof depending on whether
N=2
and
N\geq 3
.

Proof for
N=2
.

As we have already discussed how to bound the non-trivial sausage for
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})}_{z}
, occurring for
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0
, we will restrict to
\underline{b}_{1}=0
in our discussion for
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})}_{z}
. We have to consider the coefficients as shown in Figure
18
