Figure 20
:

The shapes of the initial diagrams
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}
and
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}
for
N\geq 2
. For
N=1
, these diagrams, for
x=y
(or, equivalently,
m=0
) act as bounding diagrams for
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x)
. Only the labeling for
m\leq 0
is shown. In the diagram we sum over
u,v,w,\overline{b}_{1}
, if they are present.
The lines without dash can collaps to a point, while the dashed lines include at least one step.
If
\underline{b}_{1}
is shown, then it is supposed to be unequal to zero.

In the following, we show that the sixteen
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},m}
in Figure
20
capture all possible shapes. A sausage-/rib-walk contributing to
\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}
and
\Pi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota,\kappa}_{z}
needs to satisfy that either
\overline{b}_{1}={e}_{\iota}
or
{e}_{\iota}\in A_{0}
. We bound the contribution to
\overline{b}_{1}={e}_{\iota}
using
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},m}
and
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},m}
by considering the case
\underline{b}_{1}=0
and
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0
, respectively.

The other fourteen diagrams bound contributions with
{e}_{\iota}\in A_{0}
. For these diagrams we denote by
w
the first intersection point and by
u
the last point that all paths from
0\longleftrightarrow w
and
0\longleftrightarrow{e}_{\iota}
have in common. The diagrams
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}-B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},m}
represent the case
\underline{b}_{1}=0
, where they individually describe the cases where
u=0
,
u={e}_{\iota}
and
u\not\in\{0,{e}_{\iota}\}
.

Thus, only the case
{e}_{\iota}\in A_{0}
with
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0
remains to be considered. This contribution will be bounded by
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}-B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},m}
by splitting according to the location of
u
and various other vertices involved.
In these diagrams, we consider six distinct vertices:
0
,
{e}_{\iota}
,
\underline{b}_{1}
, the first intersection point
w
, the last common vertex
u
, and the
last point
v
that the paths
0\longleftrightarrow w
and
0\longleftrightarrow\underline{b}_{1}
have in common.

We order the remaining eleven
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},m}
by the locations of
u
.

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}
-
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 9})},m}
bound the three cases
u\in\{0,v,\underline{b}_{1}\}
, while

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 7})},m}
bounds the special case where
u=\underline{b}_{1}=v
.

In the other diagrams, we put
u
on one of the four paths
0\longleftrightarrow v
,
0\longleftrightarrow\underline{b}_{1}
,
\underline{b}_{1}\longleftrightarrow v
and
v\longleftrightarrow w
, but not the endpoints of these paths (as these have already been considered in
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}
-
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 9})},m}
). To be precise

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 10})},m}
:
v=0
with
u
on the double connection between
0
and
\underline{b}_{1}
with
u\not\in\{0,\underline{b}_{1}\}
;

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 11})},m}
:
u
on connection
0\longleftrightarrow v
, for which
v\neq 0
and
u\not\in\{0,v\}
;

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 12})},m}
:
u
on connection
0\longleftrightarrow\underline{b}_{1}
, for which
v\neq 0
and
u\not\in\{0,\underline{b}_{1}\}
;

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 13})},m}
:
u
on connection
\underline{b}_{1}\longleftrightarrow v
, for which
v\neq 0
and
u\not\in\{\underline{b}_{1},v\}
;

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},m}
:
u
on connection
v\longleftrightarrow w
and
v=\underline{b}_{1}
, for which
u\neq v
;

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},m}
:
u
on connection
v\longleftrightarrow w
and
v=0
, for which
u\neq v
;

\rhd

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},m}
:
u
on connection
v\longleftrightarrow w
and
v\not\in\{0,\underline{b}_{1}\}
, for which
u\neq v
.

The restrictions on the vertices involved (such as
u\not\in\{0,v\}
for
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 11})},m}
) follow from the fact that
u\in\{0,\underline{b}_{1},v\}
were already considered in
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}
-
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 9})},m}
. We also split according to
v=0
(bounded by
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 10})},m}
and
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},m}
) and the case where
v\neq 0
(the other ones). This demonstrates that

B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}-B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},m}
really bound all possible contributions.

After this overview and validation that we have indeed considered all possible contributions, we define these diagrams formally. For
u=0
, we define, for
m=-2,\dots,2
,

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}(x,y)=\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})A^{0,m}(0,0,x,y).

The diagram
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},m}
represents the case that
u={e}_{\iota}
and is very similar to the diagrams
A^{0,m}
, defined in
Tables
4
-
7
, and are given by

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\sum_{w}\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},1,0,1}(x,w,{e}_{\iota},0)+\mathscr{S}_{2,0,0,1}(x,w,{e}_{\iota},0),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{w}\mathscr{P}_{1,\underline{1},0,0,1}(x,y,w,{e}_{\iota},0),\qquad\qquad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},1}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},-1}(y,x),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{w}\mathscr{P}_{1,2,0,0,1}(x,y,w,{e}_{\iota},0),\qquad\qquad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},2}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},-2}(y,x).

The diagrams
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},m}
represents the remaining possibilities for
u\not\in\{0,{e}_{\iota}\}
, and are defined by

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\sum_{w}\left(\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(x,w,u)+\mathscr{T}_{2,0,0}(x,w,u)\right),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\sum_{w}\mathscr{S}_{1,\underline{1},0,0}(x,y,w,u),\qquad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},1}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},-1}(y,x),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{u}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\sum_{w}\mathscr{S}_{1,2,0,0}(x,y,w,u),\qquad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},2}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},-2}(y,x).

Next we define
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},m}
, that represents the contribution of
b_{1}=(0,{e}_{\iota})
, as

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\left(\delta_{x,0}\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},3}({e}_{\iota},0)+2\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,1}({e}_{\iota},x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},0,1,1}({e}_{\iota},x,w,0)\right),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,0}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},2,\underline{1}}({e}_{\iota},y,0)+2\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},0,\underline{1},1}({e}_{\iota},y,x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},0,\underline{1},1,1}({e}_{\iota},y,x,w,0),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,0}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},0,2}({e}_{\iota},y,0)+2\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},0,2,1}({e}_{\iota},y,x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},0,2,1,1}({e}_{\iota},y,x,w,0),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},\underline{1},2}({e}_{\iota},y,0)+\delta_{x,{e}_{\iota}}\sum_{w}\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},\underline{1},1,1}({e}_{\iota},y,w,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},1,\underline{1},0,0}({e}_{\iota},x,y,w,0),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,{e}_{\iota}}\sum_{w}\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},2,0,1}({e}_{\iota},y,w,0)+\mathscr{S}_{\underline{1},1,2,0}({e}_{\iota},x,y,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},1,2,0,1}({e}_{\iota},x,y,w,0).

Further, the diagram
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},m}
bounds the case where
\overline{b}_{1}={e}_{\iota}
and
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0
, and is defined as

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle zg^{\iota}_{z}\delta_{x,y}\sum_{\kappa\neq-\iota}\mathscr{D}_{2}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{w}\big(\delta_{x,{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(w-{e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa})+\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}(x-{e}_{\iota},w-{e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa})+\mathscr{T}_{0,0,1}(w,x,{e}_{\iota})\big),

\displaystyle+zg^{\iota}_{z}\delta_{x,y}\sum_{\kappa\neq-\iota}\sum_{v,w}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa},v,0)\mathscr{T}_{0,0,0}(w-v,x-v,{e}_{\iota}-v),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle zg^{\iota}_{z}\sum_{\kappa\neq-\iota}\mathscr{D}_{2}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa})\sum_{w}\big(\mathscr{S}_{0,0,\underline{1},0}(w,y,x,{e}_{\iota})+\mathscr{S}_{0,\underline{1},0,0}(x-{e}_{\iota},y-{e}_{\iota},w-{e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa})\big)

\displaystyle+zg^{\iota}_{z}\sum_{\kappa\neq-\iota}\sum_{v,w}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa},v,0)\mathscr{S}_{0,0,\underline{1},0}(w-v,y-v,x-v,{e}_{\iota}-v),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle zg^{\iota}_{z}\sum_{\kappa\neq-\iota}\mathscr{D}_{2}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa})\sum_{w}\big(\mathscr{S}_{0,0,2,0}(w,y,x,{e}_{\iota})+\mathscr{S}_{0,2,0,0}(x-{e}_{\iota},y-{e}_{\iota},w-{e}_{\iota},{e}_{\kappa})\big)

\displaystyle+zg^{\iota}_{z}\sum_{\kappa\neq-\iota}\sum_{v,w}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}({e}_{\iota}+{e}_{\kappa},v,0)\mathscr{S}_{0,0,2,0}(w-v,y-v,x-v,{e}_{\iota}-v),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},-1}(y,x),\qquad\qquad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},2}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},-2}(y,x).

The diagram
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}
captures the contribution of
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0=u=0
. For this, we simply re-use the diagram
P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}
, and define, for
m=-2,\dots,2
,

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})(P^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}(x,y)-A^{0,m}(0,0,x,y)).\phantom{========================================================}

Then, we define
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 7})},m},B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 8})},m},B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 9})},m}
to bound the contributions due to
\underline{b}_{1}=v=u
,
\underline{b}_{1}=v\neq u
and
\underline{b}_{1}\neq v=u
, respectively, for
m=-2,\dots,2
, as

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 7})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})A^{0,m}(\underline{b}_{1},\underline{b}_{1},x,y),\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 8})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{3,1}({e}_{\iota},0)A^{-1,m}({e}_{\iota},0,x,y)+\sum_{v}\mathscr{T}_{1,1,1}({e}_{\iota},v,0)A^{-2,m}({e}_{\iota},v,x,y)

\displaystyle+\sum_{v,\underline{b}_{1}\neq{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,1}(v,\underline{b}_{1})A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 9})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{1,\underline{1},1}({e}_{\iota},\underline{b}_{1},0)A^{-1,m}(\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota},x,y)+\mathscr{T}_{1,2,1}({e}_{\iota},\underline{b}_{1},0)A^{-2,m}({e}_{\iota},\underline{b}_{1},x,y)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},v\neq{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(v,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y).

In the diagrams
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 10})},m}-B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 13})},m}
the point
u
is somewhere on the triangle
0,v,\underline{b}_{1}
, excluding the corner points, which were already considered using
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},m}-B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 9})},m}
. Thus, we define, for
m=-2,\dots,2
,

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 10})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\left(\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},u)A^{-1,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)+\mathscr{B}_{2,1}(\underline{b}_{1},u)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)\right),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 11})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{v,\underline{b}_{1}}\left(\mathscr{S}_{1,\underline{1},1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,{e}_{\iota},0)A^{-1,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)+\mathscr{S}_{1,2,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,{e}_{\iota},0)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{u,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\left(\mathscr{T}_{1,\underline{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,u)A^{-1,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)+\mathscr{T}_{1,2,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,u)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)\right),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 12})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{v,\underline{b}_{1}}\left(\mathscr{S}_{1,1,\underline{1},1}({e}_{\iota},\underline{b}_{1},v,0)A^{-1,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)+\mathscr{S}_{1,1,2,1}({e}_{\iota},\underline{b}_{1},v,0)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{u,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\left(\mathscr{T}_{1,\underline{1},1}(v,\underline{b}_{1},u)A^{-1,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)+\mathscr{T}_{1,2,1}(v,\underline{b}_{1},u)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)\right),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 13})},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{S}_{1,1,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota},v,0)A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{u,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},u)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,1}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})A^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},v,x,y).

In the diagrams
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},m}
,
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},m}
,
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},m}
, the vertex
u
is on the connection
v
to
w
,
where
u\neq v
, while
u=w
is possible. The three diagrams represent the cases
v=0
,
v=\underline{b}_{1}
and
v\neq\{0,v\}
.
Below, the first part corresponds to
u={e}_{\iota}
and the second to
u\neq{e}_{\iota}
. We thus define

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w,u}\left(\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})+\mathscr{T}_{2,1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w,u}\left(\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})+\mathscr{T}_{2,1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathscr{S}_{1,\underline{1},0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w,u}\left(\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})+\mathscr{T}_{2,1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathscr{S}_{1,2,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},2}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},-2}(y,x),

and

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},1,0,0,1}(\underline{b}_{1},x,w,{e}_{\iota},0)\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})+\mathscr{P}_{2,1,0,0,1}(\underline{b}_{1},x,w,{e}_{\iota},0)\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})\right)\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle+\delta_{x,y}\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w,u\neq{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{1,z}({e}_{\iota})\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},1,\underline{1},0,0}(\underline{b}_{1},x,y,w,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})+\mathscr{P}_{2,1,\underline{1},0,0}(\underline{b}_{1},x,y,w,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})\right)\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w,u\neq{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{S}_{1,\underline{1},0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w}\tilde{G}_{1,z}({e}_{\iota})\mathscr{P}_{\underline{1},1,2,0,0}(\underline{b}_{1},x,y,w,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w}\tilde{G}_{1,z}({e}_{\iota})\mathscr{D}_{2}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{S}_{1,2,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},w,u\neq{e}_{\iota}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{S}_{1,2,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},2}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},-2}(y,x),

Finally, we define

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\sum_{w,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(v,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle+\delta_{x,y}\sum_{u,w,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{1,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,0)\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u-v,{e}_{\iota}-v)\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{w,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(v,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{S}_{1,\underline{1},0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{u,w,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{1,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,0)\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u-v,{e}_{\iota}-v)\mathscr{S}_{1,\underline{1},0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{w,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(v,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{S}_{1,2,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{u,w,v,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{1,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,0)\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u-v,{e}_{\iota}-v)\mathscr{S}_{1,2,0,0}(x-\underline{b}_{1},y-\underline{b}_{1},w-\underline{b}_{1},u-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},2}(x,y)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},-2}(y,x).

This defines the bounding diagrams
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},m}
for
M=1,\ldots,16
and
m=-2,\ldots,2
, that bound the initial part of the diagrams for
\Xi^{\iota}
and
\Pi^{\iota,\kappa}
.

C.3
Weighted intermediate diagrams
C^{m,l}

In this section we define the weighted diagrams that we use to bound diagrams such as
\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})}}(x)
.
The shape of such diagrams is shown in the
informal definition
part of Section
5.1
.
There are
25
different cases for this diagram. Using some symmetry arguments, these reduce to
16
diagrams, that we now define one by one. We describe the bounds for LAs only, as LTs are special cases of LAs. For the bounds on LTs, we simply restrict the definitions below to LTs.

Again we use translation variance in the form that, for all
m,l\in\{-2,-1,0,1,2\}
,

\displaystyle C^{m,l}(u,v,x,y)=C^{m,l}(0,v-u,x-u,y-u).\phantom{===========================================================}

We first define
C^{0,2},C^{-2,0},C^{-2,2},C^{-2,1}
and
C^{-1,2}
as

\displaystyle C^{0,2}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=\delta_{0,v}|y|^{2}G_{z}(y)\sum_{w}\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w,x),\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle C^{-2,0}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=\delta_{x,y}|y|^{2}G_{z}(y)\sum_{w}\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-v,y-v),

\displaystyle C^{-2,2}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=|y|^{2}G_{z}(y)\sum_{w}\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-v,x-v),

\displaystyle C^{-2,1}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=2dD(x-y)|y|^{2}G_{z}(y)\sum_{w}\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-v,x-v),

\displaystyle C^{-1,2}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=2dD(v)|y|^{2}G_{z}(y)\sum_{w}\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-v,x-v).

In the following definitions of
C^{2,0},C^{2,2},C^{2,1}
and
C^{1,2}
, the weight is distributed along the line
v\rightarrow w\rightarrow y
:

\displaystyle C^{2,0}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=\delta_{x,y}\sum_{w}\tilde{G}_{1,z}(v)2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{0,y\}\}}}(|w|^{2}G_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)+|y-w|^{2}\tilde{G}_{z}(w)G_{z}(y-w)),\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle C^{2,2}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=\tilde{G}_{z}(x-v)\sum_{w}2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{0,y\}\}}}(|w|^{2}G_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)+|y-w|^{2}\tilde{G}_{z}(w)G_{z}(y-w)),

\displaystyle C^{2,1}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=2dD(x-y)\tilde{G}_{z}(x-v)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{w}2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{0,y\}\}}}(|w|^{2}G_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)+|y-w|^{2}\tilde{G}_{z}(w)G_{z}(y-w)),\phantom{===========================================================}

\displaystyle C^{1,2}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle=2dD(v)\tilde{G}_{z}(x-v)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{w}2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{0,y\}\}}}(|w|^{2}G_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)+|y-w|^{2}\tilde{G}_{z}(w)G_{z}(y-w)).

We define
C^{-1,1}
using sums over LAs, to retain as many repulsive properties as possible:

\displaystyle C^{-1,1}(0,v,x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g_{z}}(2d)^{2}D(v)D(x-y)|x|^{2}\sum_{A_{1}\ni v,A_{2}\ni y}\sum_{A_{3}\colon 0,x\in A_{3}}z^{|A_{1}|+|A_{2}|+|A_{3}|},

where
A_{1},A_{2}
are planted animals while
A_{3}
is a LA, and
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}
are such that (a)
A_{1},A_{2}
share at least one vertex, while (b)
A_{1},A_{3}
and
A_{2},A_{3}
do not share a vertex. Similarly, we define
C^{1,1}
as

\displaystyle C^{1,1}(0,v,x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g_{z}}(2d)^{2}D(v)D(x-y)\sum_{A_{1},A_{2}}\sum_{A_{3}\colon 0,x\in A_{3}}z^{|A_{1}|+|A_{2}|+|A_{3}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{w\in A_{1},w\in A_{2}}2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{v,y\}\}}}\Big({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{v\in A_{1},y\in A_{2}\}}|v-w|^{2}+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{v\in A_{2},y\in A_{1}\}}|y-w|^{2}\Big),

where now
A_{2},A_{3}
are planted animals while
A_{1}
is a LA, and
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}
are such that (a)
w
denote the first intersection point between
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, while (b)
A_{1},A_{3}
and
A_{2},A_{3}
do not share a vertex. These definitions allow us to make maximal use of repulsiveness between the lines in the bounding diagram. If we discard that repulsiveness, then we arrive instead at the bounds

\displaystyle C^{-1,-1}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle\leq(2d)^{2}D(v)D(x-y)|x|^{2}G_{z}(x)\sum_{w}\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-v,y-v),

\displaystyle C^{1,1}(0,v,x,y)

\displaystyle\leq(2d)^{2}D(v)D(x-y)\tilde{G}_{1,z}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{w}2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{v,y\}\}}}(|w|^{2}G_{z}(w)\tilde{G}_{z}(y-w)+|y-w|^{2}\tilde{G}_{z}(w)G_{z}(y-w)).

Including the repulsiveness allows us to reduce the bounds on these building blocks by about 45%, which is significant.

Each connection in our repulsive diagrams
\mathscr{B},\mathscr{T},\mathscr{S}
characterizes a planted animal.
For the following bounds, the line that carries the spatial weight
|x|^{2}
should correspond to a regular (i.e., a non-planted) animal.
For this we modify Definitions
4.6
and
4.7
, so that the initial and last sausage of one line might be non-trivial, see part (4) of Definition
4.6
. When we ignore repulsiveness, this line will correspond to a regular two-point function
\bar{G}_{z}
.

Instead of repeating Definition
4.6
, let us just mention the differences that arise in it:
We extend the index range
j_{i}\in\{l_{i},\underline{l}_{i}\}
corresponding to lines of at least
l_{i}
and exactly
\underline{l}_{i}
bonds, respectively, to
j_{i}\in\{l_{i},\underline{l}_{i},\bar{l}_{i},\bar{\underline{l}}_{i}\}
.
Here, the indices
\bar{l}_{i},\bar{\underline{l}}_{i}
correspond to the line being a regular LA, and the number of bonds being at least
\bar{l}_{i}
, respectively, exactly equal to
\bar{\underline{l}}_{i}
.
In more detail, in Definition
4.6
(2),
l_{i}
and
\bar{l}_{i}
both enforce that
|p^{i}|\leq l_{i}
, while

\underline{l}_{i}
and
\underline{\bar{l}}_{i}
both enforce that
|p^{i}|=l_{i}
. Further, we drop the constraint in Definition
4.6
(4) when
j_{i}\in\{\bar{l}_{i},\bar{\underline{l}}_{i}\}
, so that the sausage-walk

\omega_{i}
corresponds to a regular LA. Then, we extend Definition
4.7
using these skeletons to create diagrams alike
\mathscr{T}_{\bar{1},1,1}
and
\mathscr{S}_{1,\bar{0},1,\underline{1}}
. To summarize, the line with a bar on top of its length corresponds to a regular animal.

We use this to define
C^{0,0},C^{0,1},C^{-1,0}
and
C^{1,0}
as

\displaystyle C^{0,0}(0,v,x,y)=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\delta_{0,v}\delta_{x,y}}{g_{z}}|x|^{2}\left(2\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},2}(x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{\bar{1},1,1}(x,w,0)\right),

\displaystyle C^{0,1}(0,v,x,y)=

\displaystyle\ 2dD(x-y)\frac{\delta_{0,v}}{g_{z}}|y|^{2}\left(2\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(-y,y-x)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{\bar{1},1,1}(-y,w-y,x-y)\right),

\displaystyle C^{-1,0}(0,v,x,y)=

\displaystyle\ 2dD(v)\frac{\delta_{x,y}}{g_{z}}|x|^{2}\left(2\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(x,0)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{\bar{1},1,1}(x,w,0)\right),

\displaystyle C^{1,0}(0,v,x,y)=

\displaystyle\ 2dD(v)\delta_{x,y}|x-v|^{2}\frac{g^{\iota}_{z}}{g_{z}}\left(\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},1}(v,x)+\sum_{w}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(x,w,v)\right)

\displaystyle+2dD(v)\frac{\delta_{x,y}}{g_{z}}\sum_{w}2^{{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w\not\in\{v,x\}\}}}\left(|w-v|^{2}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,\bar{1}}(x,w,v)+|x-w|^{2}\mathscr{T}_{2,\bar{1},1}(x,w,v)\right).

The factor
1/g_{z}
is created by the normalisation of the coefficients, as introduced in (
3.30
).
We define the remaining cases of
m\in\{-2,-1,0,1,2\}
and
l\in\{-1,-2\}
using symmetry by

\displaystyle C^{m,-l}(0,v,x,y)=C^{m,l}(0,v,y,x).\phantom{===========================================================}.

C.4
Weighted initial diagrams of
\Xi
and
\Xi^{\iota}

In this section, we define all the weighted versions of the initial diagrams of
\Xi
and
\Xi^{\iota}
. These initial diagrams, without any weights, were already defined in Appendix
C.2
.

C.4.1
Weighted initial diagrams of
\Xi

Recall the discussion of
b_{1}
in Section
C.2.1
. For the weighted diagram bounding
\Xi
we first define the weighted version of the initial triangle for the case that
\underline{b}_{1}\neq 0
as

\displaystyle S^{w,0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\mathscr{D}_{1}(x)|x|^{2},

\displaystyle S^{w,-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2dD(x-y)\big(\delta_{y,0}\tilde{G}_{3,z}(x)+(1-\delta_{y,0})|x|^{2}G_{z}(x)\tilde{G}_{z}(y)\big),

\displaystyle S^{w,-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{y,0}|x|^{2}G_{2,z}(x)+(1-\delta_{y,0})|x|^{2}G_{z}(x)\tilde{G}_{z}(y).

These are indeed weighted versions of
S^{m}(x,y)
defined in Appendix
C.2.1
.
Then, we define the weighted diagram by

\displaystyle\Delta^{{\rm start},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle C^{0,m}(0,0,x,y)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{l=-2}^{0}\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(S^{w,l}(u,v)A^{l,m}(u,v,x,y)+\big(S^{m}(u,v)-\delta_{m,0}\delta_{u,0}\big)C^{l,m}(u,v,x,y)\Big).

This serves as a bound for the weighted initial diagram
\bar{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},m}
.
The first term captures the case
\underline{b}_{1}=0
. For the case
\underline{b}_{1}=u\neq 0
, we split the weight using
|x|^{2}\leq 2(|u|^{2}+|x-u|^{2})
. For LTs the second term is not required.

C.4.2
Weighted initial diagrams of
\Xi^{\iota}

In this section we define
\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}
and
\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}
, which are used to bound the initial parts of the bounding diagram of the weighted NoBLE coefficients

\displaystyle\sum_{x}|x|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{x}|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\Xi^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm N})},\iota}_{z}(x),

(C.1)

respectively. We defined it as the weighted versions of the diagrams shown in Figure
20
and indicated as
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},m}(x,y)
with
M=1,\ldots,16
, in which the path
x
to
y
is removed (so that there is no two-point function
G_{z}(y-x)
or any of its close friends). We always put the weight on the bottom part of the diagram.

Recall that unweighted versions of such diagrams were defined in Appendix
C.2.2
. There, we have explained that we need
16\times 5
of such diagrams. When weighing them, this number increases to
2\times 16\times 5
, since we need diagrams that are weighted with
|x|^{2}
as well as with
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
(see (
C.1
)). In more detail, we denote the
2\times 16
diagrams by
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)
and
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)
, where
I
corresponds to diagrams with the
|x|^{2}
weight as in the first term in (
C.1
), while
II
corresponds to the diagrams with the
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
weight as in the second term in (
C.1
). We then write

\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)=\sum_{M=1}^{16}D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y),\qquad\Delta^{\text{iota},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)=\sum_{M=1}^{16}D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y).

(C.2)

We are left to define
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y),D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm M})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)
for
M\in\{1,\ldots,16\}
and
m\in\{-2,\ldots,2\}
.

For
M=1
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})C^{0,m}(0,0,x,y),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})\left(C^{0,m}(0,0,x,y)+A^{m,0}(x,y,0,0)\right).

We use the spatial symmetry, as in (
6.23
), to improve the bound for
m=0
to

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 1})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\delta_{x,y}\left(\tilde{G}_{z}({e}_{\iota})C^{0,0}(0,0,x,x)+G_{z}({e}_{\iota})A^{0,0}(0,0,x,x)\right).

For
M=2
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{x,y}|x|^{2}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{S}_{1,0,0,\bar{0}}({e}_{\iota},w,x,0),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2dD(x-y)\frac{|x|^{2}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{S}_{0,1,0,\bar{0}}(w-y,{e}_{\iota}-y,-y,x-y),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{|x|^{2}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{S}_{0,1,0,\bar{0}}(w-y,{e}_{\iota}-y,-y,x-y),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},2}(x,y)=D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(y,x),

and
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)
in the same manner where
|x|^{2}
is replaced by
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}
.
For
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)
, we note a convenient estimate, which relies on the bound

\displaystyle\sum_{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}A\text{ animal}\\
0,{e}_{\iota},x,w_{1}\in A\end{array}}z^{|A|}\leq\sum_{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}A\text{ animal}\\
{e}_{\iota},x,w_{1}\in A\end{array}}z^{|A|}\leq\tilde{G}_{z}(w-{e}_{\iota})\bar{G}(x-{e}_{\iota}).

For example, this allows us to bound

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 2})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},0}(x,y)\leq|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\bar{G}(x-{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{z}(w-{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{z}(w-x).

This has the major advantage that we can directly use
f_{3}
to bound such diagrams. We will do so when the path connecting
{e}_{\iota}
and
x
has length at least
6
. For shorter connections we use explicit computation to improve our bounds further.

For
M=3
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\underline{1},1}(u,{e}_{\iota})C^{-1,m}(0,u,x,y)+\mathscr{B}_{2,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})C^{-2,m}(0,u,x,y),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 3})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)+2\tfrac{g^{\iota}_{z}}{g_{z}}\big(\tilde{G}_{2,z}(u-{e}_{\iota})A^{m,-1}(x,y,0,u)+\tilde{G}_{1,z}(u-{e}_{\iota})A^{m,-2}(x,y,0,u)\big).

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},2}(x,y)=D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 4})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(y,x),

For
M=5
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 5})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathscr{T}_{\bar{0},0,0}(x-{e}_{\iota},w-{e}_{\iota},\underline{b}_{1}-{e}_{\iota})+\mathscr{T}_{\bar{0},0,0}(x-{e}_{\iota},w-{e}_{\iota},0-{e}_{\iota})\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},v}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,0)|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}2dzD(\underline{b}_{1}-{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{S}_{\bar{0},0,0,0}(x-{e}_{\iota},w-{e}_{\iota},u-{e}_{\iota}),

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-y,\underline{b}_{1}-y)+\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-y,-y)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{b}_{1},v}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v,0)2dzD(\underline{b}_{1}-{e}_{\iota})|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}G(x-{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-y,v-y),

For
M=6

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)=

The factor
\tfrac{3}{2}
is created by the split of weights.
For
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)
we split the weight using
|x|^{2}\leq 2(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2})
.
For
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 6})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)
this is replaced in by
|x-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}\leq 3(|{e}_{\iota}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2})
,
so we
\tfrac{3}{2}
simply replaces the factor
2
by
3
for these contribution.

For
M=14
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})(1+|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2})G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,y-u)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,y-u)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{u,\underline{b}_{1}}\frac{|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}}{g_{z}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,0}(u-\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})\bar{A}^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},u,x,y),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 14})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},m}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{\underline{1},1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})(|\underline{b}_{1}-{e}_{\iota}|^{2}+|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2})G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,y-u)

\displaystyle+3\sum_{u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{T}_{2,1,0}(\underline{b}_{1},u,{e}_{\iota})\tilde{G}_{2,z}(\underline{b}_{1})(1+|x-\underline{b}_{1}|^{2})G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,y-u)

\displaystyle+3\sum_{u,\underline{b}_{1}}\frac{|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}}{g_{z}}|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,0}(u-\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota}-\underline{b}_{1})\bar{A}^{-2,m}(\underline{b}_{1},u,x,y).

For
M=15
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{g_{z}}\delta_{x,y}\sum_{w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{S}_{1,0,0,\bar{1}}({e}_{\iota},w,x,\underline{b}_{1})(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2})

\displaystyle+2\delta_{x,y}\sum_{w,u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{T}_{0,0,\bar{1}}(w-u,x-u,\underline{b}_{1}-u)(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2}),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 4dD(x-y)\sum_{w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}({e}_{\iota},w,y)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2})

\displaystyle+4dD(x-y)\sum_{w,u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{1,0}(w-u,y-u)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2}),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}({e}_{\iota},w,y)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2})

\displaystyle+2\sum_{w,u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{1,0}(w-u,y-u)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})2(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2}),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},2}(x,y)=D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(y,x),

and

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},0}(x,y)+3B^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},0}(x,y),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(x,y)+6dD(x-y)\sum_{w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}({e}_{\iota},w,y)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle+6dD(x-y)\sum_{w,u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,y-u)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(x,y)+3\sum_{w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{2}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{T}_{1,0,0}({e}_{\iota},w,y)\tilde{G}_{1,z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle+3\sum_{w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{P}_{1,\underline{1},1,0,0}(\underline{b}_{1}-x,-x,{e}_{\iota}-x,w-x,y-x)G_{3,z}(\underline{b}_{1})

\displaystyle+3\sum_{w,u,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{1}(\underline{b}_{1})\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,y-u)G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1}),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},2}(x,y)=D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 15})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-2}(y,x).

For
M=16
, we define

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2\delta_{x,y}\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathscr{T}_{0,0,1}(w-u,x-u,\underline{b}_{1}-u)|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+2\delta_{x,y}\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathscr{T}_{0,0,\bar{1}}(w-u,x-u,\underline{b}_{1}-u)|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2},

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 4dD(x-y)\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,x-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2},

\displaystyle+4dD(x-y)\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,x-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2},

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,x-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\bar{G}_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,x-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2},

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},2}(x,y)=D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle I},-2}(y,x),

and

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},0}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{g_{z}}\delta_{x,y}\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathscr{T}_{0,0,1}(w-u,x-u,\underline{b}_{1}-u)(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+1),

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{g_{z}}\delta_{x,y}\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1,1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathscr{T}_{0,0,\bar{1}}(w-u,x-u,\underline{b}_{1}-u)|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2},

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 3dD(x-y)\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,x-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2}+1),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 3\sum_{u,v,w,\underline{b}_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{\bar{1},1}(\underline{b}_{1},v)\mathscr{T}_{1,1,0}(v,u,{e}_{\iota})\mathscr{B}_{0,0}(w-u,x-u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times G_{z}(x-\underline{b}_{1})(|\underline{b}_{1}|^{2}+|\underline{b}_{1}-x|^{2}+1),

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-1}(y,x),\qquad\quad D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},2}(x,y)=D^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\text{\rm 16})},{\scriptscriptstyle II},-2}(y,x).

This defines all the bounding diagrams to the contributions to the weighted diagrams in (
C.1
), where the weight falls on the initial piece of the diagram.
